# 语料表格修复证据表 —— 第七批（换「怎么找候选」，判据不变）

写于 2026-10-09。共 **744** 张 / **573** 篇。

前两批按「孤立竖线 ≥2 行」筛文档，漏了两处：
① 行能挤在一行的压平表**不产生孤立竖线**；
② 我生成的 markdown 表行首是竖线，被当成「另一来源」→ **已修文档整篇被跳过**。
本批把候选改成「**有压平形态的签名**」= 存在行首的 ` |` token，并在 token 扫描里**排除正常 markdown 表的行**。**判据一个字没改。**

**只改 `agent-markdown/`，上游 `raw/*.htm` 一个字节不动。**

| # | markdown | 上游 | 表 | 类型 | 新表 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| 47 | `02_System_reference/02.03_DUT_board_design_reference/02.03.03.04_Remote_control_unit__V93000_EXA_Scale_system.md` | `351683.htm` | #1 | 合并格 | `\| LED \| Description \|⏎\|---\|---\|⏎\| UPPER/LOWER \| Indicates if DUT docking commands and statuses are for upper or lower DUT interface layer (Solution Extension (Upper) vs. Standard DUT board level (Lower)).<br><br>Only one LED must be lighted, otherwise other level is in error condition.<br><br>A blinking LED shows an error state. \|⏎\| DUT UNDOCK/DOCK \| - DOCK LED on, UNDOCK LED off : Stiffener detected, DUT board docked, pogo pins are in contact with the DUT board.<br>- DOCK LED off, UNDOCK LED on: DUT board undocked, pogo pins are retained.<br>- Both LEDs ON: DUT board docked. The pogo pins are in OPEN‑CONTACT position.<br>- One of both LEDs blinking: The expected state has not been reached after a timeout.<br>- Slow blinking LED: Error state/HD UNDOCK locked and HD DOCK locked.<br>- Fast blinking LED: Error state/hardware damage/timeout. \|⏎\| HARD UNDOCK/DOCK \| - DOCK LED on, UNDOCK and NEUTRAL LED off: Hard docking engaged<br>- DOCK LED off, UNDOCK on, NEUTRAL LED off: Hard docking disengaged<br>- DOCK LED off, UNDOCK off, and NEUTRAL LED on: Hard docking in NEUTRAL<br>- DOCK or UNDOCK LED blinking (fast): Timeout after 4 seconds not reaching the expected state<br>- DOCK LED blinking (slow) and NEUTRAL LED off: Hard docking engaged and locked. No automatic change to NEUTRAL<br>- UNDOCK LED blinking (slow) and NEUTRAL LED off: Hard docking disengaged and locked. No automatic change to NEUTRAL \|⏎\| STIFFENER DETECT \| - On: Stiffener detected<br>- OFF: No stiffener detected<br>- Blinking:<br><br>- Slow blinking: Stiffener in incorrect position (only one detect contact closed)<br>- Fast blinking: No Stiffener detected but DUT board docked \|⏎\| PURGE \| LED on indicates that PURGE kit function is enabled. \|⏎\| NEUTRAL \| LED on indicates NEUTRAL state of Hard docking. Before you can go for the HARD DOCK or HARD UNDOCK state, the hard docking mechanism must be in the HARD NEUTRAL state. \|⏎\| HARD DOCKING PIN POSITION \| For the HARD DOCKING PIN POSITION, there is a set of rectangular LEDS, one orange and one green, for each of the three docking cylinder, that indicates whether a docking pin is detected (orange) or in hard docking position (green).<br><br>All off<br><br>- No Coarse alignment pin in HD cylinder detected<br>- No HD cylinders are assembled \|⏎\| HARD DOCKING PIN POSITION \| For each cylinder: indicating the pin position for this cylinder<br><br>- Yellow: Pin detected, ready to Hard dock<br>- Green: Pin in Hard docking position<br>- All of one cylinder blinking: error state for this cylinder<br><br>slow blinking: one or more HD cylinders in undefined middle or docked position and no coarse alignment pin detected<br><br>fast blinking: one or more HD cylinders in conflicting position state (docked and undocked the same time) but with coarse alignment pin detected \|⏎\| DUT DOWN UNDOCK \| Indicates that DUT UNDOCK is enabled with test head in angled position (>20°)<br><br>Function is only settable in angled test head position and it is automatically disabled in case the test head angle is <=20°.<br><br>To enable the DUT DOWN UNDOCK function, it is only allowed in angled position (>20°), otherwise beeps indicating illegal action<br><br>The test system automatically leaves this state latest after 15 seconds. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/351683.htm`。` |

A smaller pad size will cause contact problems with the DUT interface. \|⏎\|---\|---\|⏎\| Signal pogo via drill size: \| 26 + 1/-3 mil finished hole size (~0.7 mm) \|⏎\| Minimum pogo via length: \| 13 mil (~0.33 mm) \|⏎\| Number of adjacent ground pogo vias: \| 4 \|⏎\| Signal via to ground via pitch: \| 100 mil \|⏎\| Removal of non-functional pads: \| Yes / No \|⏎\| Back drilling: \| Yes / No \|⏎\| Ground plane clearance or antipad: \| variable \|⏎\| Power plane clearance or antipad: \| >= ground plane clearance \|⏎\| Ground via thermal straps: \| No (direct connect to ground plane) \|⏎\| Hard Au plating \| Min. 30 µinches, Knoop hardness 130-200 \|` |

| 152 | `02_System_reference/02.04_Model_File_and_Licensing/02.04.01.03.02.02_POGOCABLING_section.md` | `106944.htm` | #0 | 普通 | `\| <physical_location> \| Specifies the physical channel card position within the test head and the respective control segment. For more information regarding the control segments refer to [pogo mapping](<../02.05_Test_Head_Cards/02.05.01.04_Pogo_mapping.md>).<br><br>The format of the number is CSS:<br><br>- C represents the control segment (1 to 4).<br>- SS represents the control segment slot number (01 to 18).<br><br>Refer to table [Card cage allocation based on test head type](<02.04.01.03.02.02_POGOCABLING_section.md#_106944__t_Card_cage_allocation>) below for the assignment of the control segment and the control segment slot number.<br><br>SS=09 and SS=18 address the two slots reserved for the legacy DPS cards, such as the MS-DPS. \|⏎\|---\|---\|⏎\| pogoblocks \| Specifies the pogo pin blocks. <pogoblock_list> is a double quoted string defining the ordered list of pogo blocks or pogo block ranges. Use commas (,) to specify a list, like `"101, 103"`. Use hyphens (-) to specify a pogo block range, like `"101-104"`. You can also mix pogo blocks and pogo ranges, like `"101, 210-212"`.<br><br>The total pogo block range is N MM, with N=1 to 8, MM=01 to 64. See also topic [DUT interface layout](<../02.03_DUT_board_design_reference/02.03.03.01_DUT_interface_layout.md>).<br><br>The physically addressable pogo block range depends on the selected pogo cable.<br><br>Note The order of list must correspond to the structure of the pogo cable assembly. The first pogo block of the list defines always pogo block A of the cable. The last pogo block of the list defines always the last pogo block of the cable. \|⏎\| type \| Specifies the pogo cable type.<br><br>- SD is used for the standard pogo cable with 16 channels per pogo block. Depending on the channel card, one SD pogo cable ends in 1-8 pogo blocks with 16 channels each.<br>- HD2x32 is presently not used by any channel card.<br>- HD4x32 describes a pogo cable with 32 channels per pogo block. One HD4x32 pogo cable ends in 4 pogo blocks with 32 channels each.<br>- VHD1x128 describes a PS1600 pogo cable with two VHD pogo blocks and 64 channels per pogo block that is to be used for special configurations only.<br>- VHD4x32 is not yet used. It describes a DPS128 pogo cable with four pogo blocks and 32 channels per pogo block.<br>- REDIRECT, subtype is used for special DPS pogo cables which shall work also on older software revisions.<br><br>If the software detects a "redirect" statement, the software will start, even if the information following redirect is unknown to this software revision.<br><br>The type parameter is optional. If not specified, the system defaults to SD. \|` |

| 192 | `02_System_reference/02.05_Test_Head_Cards/02.05.05.03.05.02_XPS256_in_PMU_operating_mode.md` | `354170.htm` | #0 | 普通 | `\| "IFVM" \| Force current and measure voltage.<br><br>This legacy mode is set so that voltage is forced and the required current is defined via symmetrical set current clamp values. Therefore, you must observe the [minimum clamp current distance](<02.05.05.03.07.01_XPS256_force_and_clamp_currents.md#_351117__s_minimumClampCurrentDistance>) for setting the forced current in the "IFVM" mode.<br><br>XPS256 supports also the real current forcing modes "CFVM" and "CFIM". \|⏎\|---\|---\|⏎\| "VFIM" \| Force voltage and measure current. \|⏎\| "VM_HIZ" \| High impedance voltage measurement. \|⏎\| "IFVM_UNGANGED" \| Force current and measure voltage by temporarily separating ganged channels for this measurement. \|⏎\| "VFIM_UNGANGED" \| Force voltage and measure current by temporarily separating ganged channels for this measurement. \|⏎\| "VFVM" \| Force voltage and measure voltage. \|⏎\| "CFVM" \| Force current directly and measure voltage. \|⏎\| "CFIM" \| Force current directly and measure current. \|` |

| 223 | `02_System_reference/02.05_Test_Head_Cards/02.05.05.03.10.02_XPS256_status_flags.md` | `354167.htm` | #3 | 合并格 | `\| Flag \| Description \| Description \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| Current limit disconnect \| Trigger: \| The user defined time for constant current mode was reached.<br><br>For the DPS operating mode, [DPSPINS resources](<../02.08_Setup_and_Result_Tools/02.08.04.14.02.03.07.01_DPSPINS_resources.md>) parameter [ms_const_curr_time_to_alarm](<../02.08_Setup_and_Result_Tools/02.08.04.14.02.03.07.01_DPSPINS_resources.md#_16887__ms_const_curr_time_to_alarm>) defines the delay time for a pin that is in constant current mode without interruption before this flag is triggered. Parameter [ms_const_curr_disconnect](<../02.08_Setup_and_Result_Tools/02.08.04.14.02.03.07.01_DPSPINS_resources.md#_16887__ms_const_curr_disconnect>) defines the delay time until the channel is disconnected. The parameter ms_const_curr_disconnect must be defined in the level setup to enable the disconnection in case the current limit is exceeded. Parameter [disable_const_curr_check](<../02.08_Setup_and_Result_Tools/02.08.04.14.02.03.07.01_DPSPINS_resources.md#_16887__disable_const_curr_check>) can be used to disable this status flag.<br><br>For the PMU operating mode, see [SPMU_PIN_TASK.currentClampTimeToAlarm()](<../02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.234.01.05_SPMU_PIN_TASK.currentClampTimeToAlarm.md>) and [SPMU_PIN_TASK.currentClampTimeToDisconnect()](<../02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.234.01.06_SPMU_PIN_TASK.currentClampTimeToDisconnect.md>). \|⏎\| Current limit disconnect \| Action: \| The related channel or the entire ganging group is disconnected. \|⏎\| Current limit disconnect \| Availability: \| The flag is available per channel. \|⏎\| Current limit disconnect \| API: \| [DPS_AUX_TASK.getConstantCurrentLimitDisconnect()](<../02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.43.06.06_DPS_AUX_TASK.getConstantCurrentLimitDisconnect.md>) \|⏎\| DUT board disconnect \| Trigger: \| The channel has received [DUT board disconnect shutdown](<02.05.05.03.03.06_XPS256_safety_line_and_DUT_board_disconnect.md#_354940__s_DUT_board_disconnect_shutdown>) signal from an appropriately configured trigger source. \|⏎\| DUT board disconnect \| Action: \| All XPS256 channels are disconnected. \|⏎\| DUT board disconnect \| Availability: \| The flag is available per channel. \|⏎\| DUT board disconnect \| API: \| [DPS_AUX_TASK.getDutBoardDisconnect()](<../02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.43.06.09_DPS_AUX_TASK.getDutBoardDisconnect.md>) \|⏎\| DUT board disconnect \| Note: \| See also [XPS256 programming - Safety line and DUT board disconnect](<02.05.05.03.10.18_XPS256_programming_-_Safety_line_and_DUT_board_disconnect.md>). \|⏎\| Overpower / Overcurrent / Power budget exceeded \| Trigger: \| An overpower or overcurrent condition has been detected.<br><br>The flag is triggered when the overcurrent condition has been detected for longer than 50 ms without interruption. \|⏎\| Overpower / Overcurrent / Power budget exceeded \| Action: \| The related channel is disconnected. \|⏎\| Overpower / Overcurrent / Power budget exceeded \| Availability: \| The flag is available per channel. \|⏎\| Overpower / Overcurrent / Power budget exceeded \| API: \| [DPS_AUX_TASK.getOvercurrent()](<../02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.43.06.11_DPS_AUX_TASK.getOvercurrent.md>) \|⏎\| Overtemperature \| Trigger: \| The temperature of the channel hardware is too high. \|⏎\| Overtemperature \| Action: \| The related channel is disconnected. \|⏎\| Overtemperature \| Availability: \| The flag is available per channel. \|⏎\| Overtemperature \| API: \| [DPS_AUX_TASK.getOvertemperature()](<../02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.43.06.12_DPS_AUX_TASK.getOvertemperature.md>) \|⏎\| Overvoltage \| Trigger: \| The applied voltage exceeds the maximum voltage of the active voltage range. \|⏎\| Overvoltage \| Action: \| The channel is disconnected. \|⏎\| Overvoltage \| Availability: \| The flag is available per channel. \|⏎\| Overvoltage \| API: \| [DPS_AUX_TASK.getOvervoltage()](<../02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.43.06.13_DPS_AUX_TASK.getOvervoltage.md>) \|⏎\| Safety line \| Trigger: \| The channel has received [Safety line shutdown](<02.05.05.03.03.06_XPS256_safety_line_and_DUT_board_disconnect.md#_354940__s_Safety_line_shutdown>) signal from an appropriately configured and assigned trigger source. \|⏎\| Safety line \| Action: \| Only XPS256 channels assigned to the trigger source are disconnected. \|⏎\| Safety line \| Availability: \| The flag is available per channel. \|⏎\| Safety line \| API: \| [DPS_AUX_TASK.getSafetyLine()](<../02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.43.06.15_DPS_AUX_TASK.getSafetyLine.md>) \|⏎\| Safety line \| Note: \| See also [XPS256 programming - Safety line and DUT board disconnect](<02.05.05.03.10.18_XPS256_programming_-_Safety_line_and_DUT_board_disconnect.md>). \|⏎\| Unregulated \| Trigger: \| The XPS256 channel is in an unregulated state.<br><br>The flag is triggered when the output stage is at its limits for longer than 10 ms without interruption. \|⏎\| Unregulated \| Action: \| The channel is disconnected. \|⏎\| Unregulated \| Availability: \| The flag is available per channel. \|⏎\| Unregulated \| API: \| [DPS_AUX_TASK.getUnregulated()](<../02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.43.06.16_DPS_AUX_TASK.getUnregulated.md>) \|⏎\| Voltage drop force \| Trigger: \| The voltage drop on the force (P#) line on the DUT board exceeds the user defined limit.<br><br>Default threshold for the force line is 150 mV.<br><br>The flag is set if the threshold is exceeded without interruption for the time defined by the [Vdrop Hold time](<02.05.05.03.10.05_XPS256_programming_-_Default_values.md#_354611__r_Vdrop_hold_time>) parameter for the corresponding pin. Note that the Vdrop Hold time parameter can be set only in the [DC Scale Safety editor](<02.05.05.03.10.05_XPS256_programming_-_Default_values.md>). The time can be set separately for the force line and for the return line.<br><br>For XPS256 channels in DPS operating mode, see [max_voltage_drop_force](<../02.08_Setup_and_Result_Tools/02.08.04.14.02.03.07.01_DPSPINS_resources.md#_16887__max_voltage_drop_force>) in [DPSPINS resources](<../02.08_Setup_and_Result_Tools/02.08.04.14.02.03.07.01_DPSPINS_resources.md>).<br><br>For XPS256 channels in PMU operating mode, see [SPMU_PIN_TASK.forceDropMax()](<../02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.234.01.09_SPMU_PIN_TASK.forceDropMax.md>). \|⏎\| Voltage drop force \| Action: \| By default, the channel remains connected.<br><br>The channel is disconnected if the [Vdrop Disco](<02.05.05.03.10.05_XPS256_programming_-_Default_values.md#_354611__r_Vdrop_Disco>) parameter is set to ON for the corresponding pin. Note that the Vdrop Disco parameter can be set only in the [DC Scale Safety editor](<02.05.05.03.10.05_XPS256_programming_-_Default_values.md>). \|⏎\| Voltage drop force \| Availability: \| The flag is available per channel. \|⏎\| Voltage drop force \| API: \| [DPS_AUX_TASK.getVoltageDropForce()](<../02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.43.06.17_DPS_AUX_TASK.getVoltageDropForce.md>) \|⏎\| Voltage drop return \| Trigger: \| The voltage drop on the common return line (GND) on the DUT board exceeds the user defined limit.<br><br>Default threshold for the return line is 50 mV.<br><br>The flag is set if the threshold is exceeded without interruption for the time defined by the [Vdrop Hold time](<02.05.05.03.10.05_XPS256_programming_-_Default_values.md#_354611__r_Vdrop_hold_time>) parameter for the corresponding pin. Note that the Vdrop Hold time parameter can be set only in the [DC Scale Safety editor](<02.05.05.03.10.05_XPS256_programming_-_Default_values.md>). The time can be set separately for the force line and for the return line.<br><br>For XPS256 channels in DPS operating mode, see [max_voltage_drop_return](<../02.08_Setup_and_Result_Tools/02.08.04.14.02.03.07.01_DPSPINS_resources.md#_16887__max_voltage_drop_return>) in [DPSPINS resources](<../02.08_Setup_and_Result_Tools/02.08.04.14.02.03.07.01_DPSPINS_resources.md>).<br><br>For XPS256 channels in PMU operating mode, see [SPMU_PIN_TASK.returnDropMax()](<../02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.234.01.29_SPMU_PIN_TASK.returnDropMax.md>). \|⏎\| Voltage drop return \| Action: \| By default, the channel remains connected.<br><br>The channel is disconnected if the [Vdrop Disco](<02.05.05.03.10.05_XPS256_programming_-_Default_values.md#_354611__r_Vdrop_Disco>) parameter is set to ON for the corresponding pin. Note that the Vdrop Disco parameter can be set only in the [DC Scale Safety editor](<02.05.05.03.10.05_XPS256_programming_-_Default_values.md>). \|⏎\| Voltage drop return \| Availability: \| The flag is available per channel. \|⏎\| Voltage drop return \| API: \| [DPS_AUX_TASK.getVoltageDropReturn()](<../02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.43.06.19_DPS_AUX_TASK.getVoltageDropReturn.md>) \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/354167.htm`。` |

| 247 | `02_System_reference/02.05_Test_Head_Cards/02.05.05.03.10.15_XPS256_programming_-_PMU_operating_mode.md` | `354253.htm` | #5 | 合并格 | `\| Parameter mode \| Description \| Description \| Note \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| "VFVM" \| Force voltage and measure voltage. \|  \| Basic voltage or current forcing modes of the PMU with programmable current or voltage clamp.<br><br>Note: Unlike other DC scale cards, XPS256 pins feature a real current force mode with programmable voltage clamps. \|⏎\| "VFIM" \| Force current and measure voltage. \|  \| Basic voltage or current forcing modes of the PMU with programmable current or voltage clamp.<br><br>Note: Unlike other DC scale cards, XPS256 pins feature a real current force mode with programmable voltage clamps. \|⏎\| "VFIM_UNGANGED" \| Force voltage and measure current by temporarily separating ganged channels for this measurement. \|  \| Basic voltage or current forcing modes of the PMU with programmable current or voltage clamp.<br><br>Note: Unlike other DC scale cards, XPS256 pins feature a real current force mode with programmable voltage clamps. \|⏎\| "CFVM" \| Force current directly and measure voltage. \|  \| Basic voltage or current forcing modes of the PMU with programmable current or voltage clamp.<br><br>Note: Unlike other DC scale cards, XPS256 pins feature a real current force mode with programmable voltage clamps. \|⏎\| "CFIM" \| Force current directly and measure current. \|  \| Basic voltage or current forcing modes of the PMU with programmable current or voltage clamp.<br><br>Note: Unlike other DC scale cards, XPS256 pins feature a real current force mode with programmable voltage clamps. \|⏎\| "IFVM" \| Force current and measure voltage. \|  \| Legacy mode for compatibility with other DC scale cards: Currents are forced using the clamp current functionality. The clamp current is programmed to the required force value, and the voltage must be set to a value that forces the pin into the clamping situation. \|⏎\| "IFVM_UNGANGED" \| Force current and measure voltage by temporarily separating ganged channels for this measurement.<br><br>Voltage measurements in unganged mode means that the number of forcing channels of a ganging group is minimized temporarily to improve the stability of the forced current and the voltage measurement. \|  \| Legacy mode for compatibility with other DC scale cards: Currents are forced using the clamp current functionality. The clamp current is programmed to the required force value, and the voltage must be set to a value that forces the pin into the clamping situation. \|⏎\| "VM_HIZ" \| High impedance voltage measurement. \|  \| The PMU is set to a high-impedance mode. Only voltage measurements are supported. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/354253.htm`。` |

| 255 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.111_ERCE__-__Expected_and_Received_Cycle_data_of_Events.md` | `118682.htm` | #4 | 合并格 | `\| Content \| Bits \| Description \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| Compare result \| 0 (LSB) \| Upper limit. This flag is set to 1 if all of the following conditions are satisfied:<br><br>- An upper limit is specified (limitHigh attribute of measure operation).<br>- The check of the upper limit is active (failAboveHighLimit attribute of measure operation set to true with DFTD).<br>- The measurement result is not masked (maskPassFail attribute of the measure operation set to false with DFTD).<br>- The measured value is greater than the upper limit. \|⏎\| Compare result \| 1 \| Lower limit. This flag is set to 1 if all of the following conditions are satisfied:<br><br>- A lower limit is specified (limitLow attribute of measure operation).<br>- The check of the lower limit is active (failBelowLowLimit attribute of measure operation set to true with DFTD).<br>- The measurement result is not masked (maskPassFail attribute of the measure operation set to false with DFTD).<br>- The measured value is smaller than the low limit. \|⏎\| Compare result \| 2 \| Status information. This flag is set to 1 if at least one of the following conditions is satisfied:<br><br>- One of the bits 3 through 5 is set to 1 and active, and the measurement result is not masked.<br>- Bit 6 is set to 1<br>- Bit 7 is set to 1 \|⏎\| Status information \| 3 \| Low clamp violation. This flag is set to 1 if there is a low clamp violation.<br><br>But note that it is evaluated for the status information (bit 2) only if the following additional conditions are satisfied:<br><br>- The check of low clamp violation is active (failOnLowClamp attribute of measure operation set to true with DFTD)<br>- The measurement result is not masked (maskPassFail attribute of the measure operation set to false). \|⏎\| Status information \| 4 \| High clamp violation. This flag is set to 1 if there is a high clamp violation.<br><br>But note that it is evaluated for the status information (bit 2) only if the following additional conditions are satisfied:<br><br>- The check of high clamp violation is active (failOnHighClamp attribute of measure operation set to true with DFTD).<br>- The measurement result is not masked (maskPassFail attribute of the measure operation set to false). \|⏎\| Status information \| 5 \| Measurement range exceeded. This flag is set to 1 if the measurement range is exceeded.<br><br>But note that it is evaluated for the status information (bit 2) only if the following additional conditions are satisfied:<br><br>- The check of the measurement range is active (failOnOutOfRange attribute of measure operation set to true).<br>- The measurement result is not masked (maskPassFail attribute of the measure operation set to false). \|⏎\| Status information \| 6 \| Distance too small. This flag is set to 1 if the distance between two consecutive measurements is too small. It is always evaluated for the status information (bit 2), even if the measurement result is masked. \|⏎\| Status information \| 7 (MSB) \| Type mismatch. This flag is set to 1 if the measurement type does not match the measurement range. It is always evaluated for the status information (bit 2), even if the measurement result is masked. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/118682.htm`。` |
| 256 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.115_ERRE__-__Expected_and_Received_Results_of_Events.md` | `97182.htm` | #0 | 合并格 | `\| type \| The supported types are:<br><br>- RCV<br><br>Requests received data only.<br><br>Can only be requested if a test point pattern has been executed for DC Scale pins, or a DC pattern was executed for digital pins.<br><br>- EXP<br><br>Requests expected data of only those test point events that generate a result.<br><br>This type is only allowed for test point patterns, not for DC patterns.<br><br>- ALL<br><br>Requests both expected and received data.<br><br>This type is only allowed for test points patterns, not for DC patterns. \|⏎\|---\|---\|⏎\| resultIndex \| The index of the first result to be read. Only test point operations (MEAS instructions) that provide a result are counted, starting from 0. \|⏎\| numResults \| The number of results to be retrieved. \|⏎\| data_format \| Specifies the binary data format of #spec(data). The DC Scale cards DPS32, VI32, and UHC4 / UHC4T, as well as DC patterns, support both formats.<br><br>- `0`<br><br>This format contains PASS/FAIL and status information for every measurement. It can be used for status analysis where expected data and measurement values are not required. This format is only available for type RCV.<br><br>- `1`<br><br>For test point events, this format can return both received and expected data. Expected data are not available for DC patterns.<br><br>The received data of format 1 not only contains PASS/FAIL and status information for every measurement, but also the measurement type and value.<br><br>The expected data contains all the setup information of an event (td element) that contains a Measure operation.<br><br>Format 1 is available for all three types. \|⏎\| rep_mode \| Specifies the reporting mode. Possible values are:<br><br>- ALL:Reports all results within the selected area.<br>- EOLY: Reports only failed results including measurement errors (that is, too small a distance between two measurements, or type mismatch between measurement and range). This mode is not available for type EXP. \|⏎\| byte_max \| In the query, this parameter specifies the maximum number of bytes that may be returned. This option can be used for the reporting mode EOLY, where the size of the data that will be returned cannot be calculated beforehand.<br><br>In the reply, this parameter is used to indicate the reason in case that not all the requested results can be returned:<br><br>- EOB<br><br>The number of requested results exceeds the maximum buffer length specified with byte_max.<br><br>- EOF<br><br>The result range of the query exceeds the length of the test.<br><br>- EOM<br><br>This code is returned if the size of the answer exceeds the available storage capacity. \|⏎\| gang_index \| The index within the ganged channels for which data will be<br><br>answered. If this parameter is used, the connect_state (set with [PSLR](<02.06.02.02.194_PSLR__PSLR__-__Power_Supply_Level_Ranges.md>)) must be hdig or norm_hdig.<br><br>This only applies to test point measurements. \|⏎\| pinlist \| DC Scale pins for which no test point pattern has been executed and digital pins for which no DC pattern has been executed will be ignored. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| The binary data that are returned. The general format is:<br><br>#Nn1n2n...nNdata_stream<br><br>The N digit defines the number of the digits ni (1 ≤ i ≤N) that specify the length of data_stream in bytes.<br><br>The data stream itself consists of a stream header and one or more answers (only one answer for data formats 0 and 1), where every answer contains the same number of data blocks.<br><br>The stream header defines<br><br>- the number of answers (4 long bytes, big-endian encoded)<br>- the number of data blocks per answer (4 long bytes, big-endian encoded).<br><br>Every data block consists of a block header and one or more data units each of which contains the result of one test point measurement or MEAS instruction (DC pattern).<br><br>The block header defines<br><br>- the index of the first result (4 long bytes, big-endian encoded)<br>- the number of successive results (4 long bytes, big-endian encoded).<br><br>If rep_mode is set to ALL, there will be only one block; if it is set to EOLY, there can be more than one.<br><br>The length of the data units depends on the selected format and on the selected type. If type is set to ALL, each data unit consists of the expected data and the received data in this order.<br><br>Graphically, the structure can be illustrated by a simple example of the EOLY reporting mode with type RCV as follows: \|⏎\| ![Format of data stream](<../../../../raw/101276.png>) \| ![Format of data stream](<../../../../raw/101276.png>) \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/97182.htm`。` |

| 258 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.122_FFCI__-__First_Failed_Cycle_Information.md` | `98603.htm` | #0 | 普通 | `\| no_ffcyc \| Number of first failed cycles to be reported. \|⏎\|---\|---\|⏎\| port \| Specifies the port for the pins for which information is to be returned. If you omit this parameter information will be returned for the default port @ to which all configured pins belong. \|⏎\| no_pins \| The number of O and IO pins. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| Prefix and pin error data in binary format:<br><br>- The prefix is defined as follows:<br><br>- The number sign # marks the start of the data block.<br>- One digit follows, indicating how many digits make up the following data length specifier.<br>- A number of digits indicating the length (in bytes) of the data to follow.<br><br>- Binary data are available per reported cycle. The data for each cycle consists of two parts, the number of the failing cycle and the pin data, which indicates the failing pins of that cycle:<br><br>- Cycle number:<br><br>The length of the cycle number is always 8 bytes.<br><br>- Pin data:<br><br>The pin data consists of one byte per eight pins that belong to the specified port:<br><br>- Each pin is represented by one bit.<br>- An error on a particular pin is denoted by a 1, an error free pin by a 0.<br>- Bits of the pin data are filled starting from the left (MSB) side.<br>- If the number of configured channels of the specified port is not divisible by 8, the last byte of pin data contains unused bits on the right (LSB) side.<br><br>Mapping of the bits in the pin data block to the individual pins:<br><br>- The I / IO pins are sorted based on their pogo number defined for the first site.<br>- The sorted pins are mapped starting on the left (MSB) side of the first byte of the pin data block.<br>- The information how the pins are mapped can be read by command [FFPM? (First Failing cycle information Pin Mapping)](<02.06.11.02.24_FFPM___First_Failing_cycle_information_Pin_Mapping.md>). \|` |

| 260 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.162_MSCF__MSCF__-__Modulation_Set_ConFiguration.md` | `143671.htm` | #1 | 合并格 | `\| Property \| Explanation and values \| Explanation and values \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| SHAPE \| This parameter either<br><br>(a) defines a list of offsets (in picoseconds) from the edge delays specified in the currently active timing set, or<br><br>(b) selects a predefined waveform.<br><br>In case (a), the list items must be separated by a comma, and the maximum number of items is 3072. Note that case (a) is only supported for STDDIFF pins.<br><br>The list items of case (a) do not define the actual delays, but only their mutual proportions. The actual values are computed from the list and the required maximum value (AMPLITUDE property) by<br><br>list_item * ( max_value / max_item),<br><br>where max_item is the greatest item in the specified list. To give a simple example, if the specified list is<br><br>0, 10, 20, 10, 0, -5, -10, -5, 0<br><br>and the required maximum value is 30ps, then the actual waveform is calculated by<br><br>list_item * ( 30 / 20)<br><br>as<br><br>0, 15, 30, 15, 0, -7.5, -15, -7.5, 0<br><br>picoseconds. If no amplitude is specified, the default of 100ps is assumed.<br><br>The predefined waveforms (case (b)) are: \| This parameter either<br><br>(a) defines a list of offsets (in picoseconds) from the edge delays specified in the currently active timing set, or<br><br>(b) selects a predefined waveform.<br><br>In case (a), the list items must be separated by a comma, and the maximum number of items is 3072. Note that case (a) is only supported for STDDIFF pins.<br><br>The list items of case (a) do not define the actual delays, but only their mutual proportions. The actual values are computed from the list and the required maximum value (AMPLITUDE property) by<br><br>list_item * ( max_value / max_item),<br><br>where max_item is the greatest item in the specified list. To give a simple example, if the specified list is<br><br>0, 10, 20, 10, 0, -5, -10, -5, 0<br><br>and the required maximum value is 30ps, then the actual waveform is calculated by<br><br>list_item * ( 30 / 20)<br><br>as<br><br>0, 15, 30, 15, 0, -7.5, -15, -7.5, 0<br><br>picoseconds. If no amplitude is specified, the default of 100ps is assumed.<br><br>The predefined waveforms (case (b)) are: \|⏎\|  \| SINE \| Creates a sinusoidal waveform. \|⏎\|  \| RANDOM \| Generates random jitter within delay - amplitude and delay + amplitude,<br><br>where delay is the delay that is defined in the currently active timing set for the pins that are associated with the modulation set in the respective jitter set, and amplitude is the value of the AMPLITUDE property. \|⏎\| PHASE \| This property is intended to define a phase offset for the RESTART mode (the mode is defined with JSCF). This value must be specified as a degree. The maximum is 360°. \| This property is intended to define a phase offset for the RESTART mode (the mode is defined with JSCF). This value must be specified as a degree. The maximum is 360°. \|⏎\| AMPLITUDE \| This property determines the amplitude (that is, half of the peak-to-peak value) of a predefined shape.<br><br>The maximum is 200 ps. The default is always 100 ps. \| This property determines the amplitude (that is, half of the peak-to-peak value) of a predefined shape.<br><br>The maximum is 200 ps. The default is always 100 ps. \|⏎\| PERIOD \| This property defines the jitter period in picoseconds.<br><br>In general, the maximum period depends on the clock resolution as follows:<br><br>2 * clock_resolution * 1024 * 512<br><br>The clock resolution is calculated from the period resolution, but also takes other parameters into account. Therefore a different clock resolution may be calculated from the same period resolution in different circumstances.<br><br>If you want to have complete control over the clock resolution, you must specify a period resolution not greater than 2 * minimal_clock_resolution manually (with the period_res keyword). Then the clock resolution will equal the period resolution. It is used to calculate the size of the result area and to determine how the measurements are to be processed. The possible values are 1 or 2. Two shots are needed for rise time and fall time measurements. The default is 1. \|⏎\| PRESCALER \| This mandatory property defines how much the frequency of the signal is to be reduced before it is passed on to the event discarding block. This is required if the minimal distance of two consecutive events in the DUT signal can be smaller than the minimal distance that the event discarding block supports.<br><br>Possible values for Smart Scale pins are 1 through 17. 1 means no prescaling. If EDGE_SELECT is set to RISE_FALL, the prescaler value must be < 17 and > 1. \| This mandatory property defines how much the frequency of the signal is to be reduced before it is passed on to the event discarding block. It is only needed for (two shot) rise time and fall time measurements. The default is the voltage level as defined in the level setup.<br><br>Note that if this property is defined, the level setup must be changed, and if the pins specified by pinlist are tested functionally in parallel with the TMU measurement, they will be tested with the modified voltage level.<br><br>Note also that the original level setup is automatically restored after the measurement has been finished. \| This optional property defines a voltage threshold for the event to be sampled. | 267 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.02.02.287_TTDB__-__Transition_Test_DeBug_information.md` | `116688.htm` | #1 | 合并格 | `\| Information type \| Explanation and values \| Explanation and values \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| MAX_DEVIATION \| If the token size was > 0 during the last sequencer run, this item returns for this sequencer run:<br><br>- The maximum number of UI by which the received data has fallen behind the exceeded the expected data (subtype DUT_BEHIND_ED)<br>- The maximum number of UI by which the expected data has fallen behind the received data (subtype ED_BEHIND_DUT)<br><br>The received data is shortened by skipping idle tokens, the expected data by removing X-tokens. \| If the token size was > 0 during the last sequencer run, this item returns for this sequencer run:<br><br>- The maximum number of UI by which the received data has fallen behind the exceeded the expected data (subtype DUT_BEHIND_ED)<br>- The maximum number of UI by which the expected data has fallen behind the received data (subtype ED_BEHIND_DUT)<br><br>The received data is shortened by skipping idle tokens, the expected data by removing X-tokens. \|⏎\| TRK_FIRST_ADJUSTMENT \| This item returns the adjustment that was determined by the first transition after test start. \| This item returns the adjustment that was determined by the first transition after test start. \|⏎\| TRK_FIRST_ERR \| This item returns the number of expected bits that had been processed when the first tracking error occurred. \| This item returns the number of expected bits that had been processed when the first tracking error occurred. \|⏎\| TRK_VALUES \| This item returns information about the adjustment of the observation window. The unit is 0.25 UI (= 1 sample).<br><br>There are three subtypes:<br><br>- ACT returns the actual adjustment at the end of the test.<br>- MIN returns the maximum adjustment to the left during the last test.<br>- MAX returns the maximum adjustment to the right during the last test. \| This item returns information about the adjustment of the observation window. The unit is 0.25 UI (= 1 sample).<br><br>There are three subtypes:<br><br>- ACT returns the actual adjustment at the end of the test.<br>- MIN returns the maximum adjustment to the left during the last test.<br>- MAX returns the maximum adjustment to the right during the last test. \|⏎\| TRK_STATUS \| This item returns the transition patterns that were observed during the last test as a sequence of four-digit blocks.<br><br>In order to understand this information, you must take into account that the observation window can only be adjusted after every three UI (see the discussion of the implementation parameters in [Drift and transition tracking](<02.06.15.01.01_Drift_and_transition_tracking.md>)). Thus, the observation window actually consists of three successive UI.<br><br>The four-digit blocks encode the accumulated transitions for one such 3-UI window (subtype TRANS_COMPOSITE) or for two such windows (subtype TRANS_X). "1" indicates a transition, "0" indicates no transition. Every block starts with "1". This indicates that the observation window was adjusted successfully.<br><br>As an example for the one window case, consider<br><br>LLLL LHHH LLLH,<br><br>where the second UI has an AB transition, and the third has a CD transition. This is encoded by<br><br>1101,<br><br>which says that an AB transition (second digit) and a CD transition (fourth digit), but no BC transition was observed in the 3-UI window.<br><br>TRK_STATUS has three subtypes:<br><br>- TRANS_X returns the transition patterns that occurred in the last 24 UI (= 8 windows, each of 3 UI). Each of the four blocks accumulates two windows (= 6 UI). The order of the blocks corresponds to the order of the test.<br>- TRANS_COMPOSITE returns the transition patterns that occurred by accumulating the 3 UI of one window during the last sequencer run up to the first error. Since the first digit is always 1 (except when the window could not be adjusted) there are 8 possible patterns between 1000 (best) and 1111 (worst). The patterns that occurred are displayed.<br>- TRANS_R returns 0 if the observation window could always be adjusted successfully. It returns 1 if this is not the case. This error should only occur if the observation window was adjusted more than 1 UI in the same direction within 24 UI (see the ERR_CNTADJOVR property of the TTCF command). \| This item returns the transition patterns that were observed during the last test as a sequence of four-digit blocks.<br><br>In order to understand this information, you must take into account that the observation window can only be adjusted after every three UI (see the discussion of the implementation parameters in [Drift and transition tracking](<02.06.15.01.01_Drift_and_transition_tracking.md>)). Thus, the observation window actually consists of three successive UI.<br><br>The four-digit blocks encode the accumulated transitions for one such 3-UI window (subtype TRANS_COMPOSITE) or for two such windows (subtype TRANS_X). "1" indicates a transition, "0" indicates no transition. Every block starts with "1". This indicates that the observation window was adjusted successfully.<br><br>As an example for the one window case, consider<br><br>LLLL LHHH LLLH,<br><br>where the second UI has an AB transition, and the third has a CD transition. This is encoded by<br><br>1101,<br><br>which says that an AB transition (second digit) and a CD transition (fourth digit), but no BC transition was observed in the 3-UI window.<br><br>TRK_STATUS has three subtypes:<br><br>- TRANS_X returns the transition patterns that occurred in the last 24 UI (= 8 windows, each of 3 UI). Each of the four blocks accumulates two windows (= 6 UI). The order of the blocks corresponds to the order of the test.<br>- TRANS_COMPOSITE returns the transition patterns that occurred by accumulating the 3 UI of one window during the last sequencer run up to the first error. Since the first digit is always 1 (except when the window could not be adjusted) there are 8 possible patterns between 1000 (best) and 1111 (worst). The patterns that occurred are displayed.<br>- TRANS_R returns 0 if the observation window could always be adjusted successfully. It returns 1 if this is not the case. This error should only occur if the observation window was adjusted more than 1 UI in the same direction within 24 UI (see the ERR_CNTADJOVR property of the TTCF command). \|⏎\| LOCATION_FIRST_FAIL \| This item returns the number of expected bits that had been processed when the first error of any kind occurred. \| This item returns the number of expected bits that had been processed when the first error of any kind occurred. \|⏎\| REMOVED_IDLE \| This item returns how often one of the 6 idle tokens that had been defined with TTCF IDLE_TOKEN_i occurred during the last sequencer run. The sub_type parameter specifies the number of the token. \| This item returns how often one of the 6 idle tokens that had been defined with TTCF IDLE_TOKEN_i occurred during the last sequencer run. The sub_type parameter specifies the number of the token. \|⏎\| REMOVED_XTOKEN \| This item returns how many X-tokens were removed. This only applies if X-token removal is activated with TTCF REMOVE_XTOKEN_MODE.<br><br>REMOVED_XTOKEN has one subtype:<br><br>- SEQUENCER_RUN returns the number of X-tokens that were removed on the last sequencer run \| This item returns how many X-tokens were removed. This only applies if X-token removal is activated with TTCF REMOVE_XTOKEN_MODE.<br><br>REMOVED_XTOKEN has one subtype:<br><br>- SEQUENCER_RUN returns the number of X-tokens that were removed on the last sequencer run \|⏎\| STATUS \| This item returns information about the state of the tracker after the test as a bit sequence. Most of these status bits can also be queried with TTST?. The individual bits stand for: \| This item returns information about the state of the tracker after the test as a bit sequence. Most of these status bits can also be queried with TTST?. The individual bits stand for: \|⏎\| STATUS \| - Bit1: An interrupt was generated.<br>- Bit2: Break vectors entered the formatting block.<br>- Bit3: The first match between expected and received data has occurred.<br>- Bit4: Expected and received data are prepared for the first compare.<br>- Bit5: The FIFO between tracking and pattern matching block is empty.<br>- Bit6: The FIFO between expected data and pattern matching block is empty.<br>- Bit7: The expected data window FIFO is empty.<br>- Bit8: The expected data FIFO is empty.<br>- Bit9: EXPECTED_FIFO_DUT_OVERFLOW<br>- Bit10: ADJUSTMENT_ERROR<br>- Bit11: Always 1<br>- Bit12: DRIVE_BIT_IN_COMPARE_DATA<br>- Bit13: DUT_FIFO_OVERFLOW<br>- Bit14: ERR_XCNT \| - Bit15: ERR_XMIN<br>- Bit16: ERR_XMAX<br>- Bit17: ERR_TRMCNT<br>- Bit18: ERR_WRCMP<br>- Bit19: ERR_COMPARE<br>- Bit20: ERR_STO<br>- Bit21: ERR_FTO<br>- Bit22: ERR_STOP<br>- Bit23: ERR_CNTLMT<br>- Bit24: ERR_CNTADJOVR<br>- Bit25: ERR_BADTRAN<br>- Bit26: ERR_PULSE<br>- Bit27: ERR_TRK<br>- Bit28: ERR_JMP2<br>- Bit29: ERR_TE4<br>- Bit30: ERR_TE0<br>- Bit31: ERR_TE2<br>- Bit32: ERR_TE3 \|⏎\| STATUS \| The names specified for bits 9 through 32 refer to the possible errors returned by [TTST?](<02.06.02.02.292_TTST__-__Transition_Test_STatus_information.md>). For more explanations see this query and the [TTCF](<02.06.02.02.286_TTCF__TTCF__-__Transition_Test_ConFiguration.md>) command. \| The names specified for bits 9 through 32 refer to the possible errors returned by [TTST?](<02.06.02.02.292_TTST__-__Transition_Test_STatus_information.md>). For more explanations see this query and the [TTCF](<02.06.02.02.286_TTCF__TTCF__-__Transition_Test_ConFiguration.md>) command. \|⏎\| REMOVED_XTOKEN_EMAP \| This item returns how many x-tokens were removed with respect to start and stop of the error map.<br><br>REMOVED_XTOKEN_EMAP has two subtypes:<br><br>- START returns the number of removed X-tokens at the start of the error map.<br>- STOP returns the number of removed X-tokens at the end of error map recording. \| This item returns how many x-tokens were removed with respect to start and stop of the error map.<br><br>REMOVED_XTOKEN_EMAP has two subtypes:<br><br>- START returns the number of removed X-tokens at the start of the error map.<br>- STOP returns the number of removed X-tokens at the end of error map recording. \|⏎\| SYNC_VALUES \| This item returns how many received bits were skipped for initial synchronization at test start (subtype INITIAL) or after a switch of an IO pin from drive to receive mode (subtype SUBSEQUENT). \| This item returns how many received bits were skipped for initial synchronization at test start (subtype INITIAL) or after a switch of an IO pin from drive to receive mode (subtype SUBSEQUENT). \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/116688.htm`。` |

The following rules apply to these values:<br><br>- A waveform that is used for selective digital capture cannot contain more than one compare action. Therefore, if you specify C or D for one of ED1 or ED2, you cannot specify a value for the other Expected Data parameter.<br>- If you issue more than one instance of the WAVE command for the same logical waveform, the values you specify for the same Expected Data parameter in these different WAVE instances must be homogeneous; that is, either all or none of them must be C or D. Therefore, if you specify C or D for one of ED1 or ED2, you cannot specify a value for the other Expected Data parameter.<br>- If you issue more than one instance of the WAVE command for the same logical waveform, the values you specify for the same Expected Data parameter in these different WAVE instances must be homogeneous; that is, either all or none of them must be C or D. | 290 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.11.02.23_FFCI__-__First_Failed_Cycle_Information.md` | `98603_2.htm` | #0 | 普通 | `\| no_ffcyc \| Number of first failed cycles to be reported. \|⏎\|---\|---\|⏎\| port \| Specifies the port for the pins for which information is to be returned. If you omit this parameter information will be returned for the default port @ to which all configured pins belong. \|⏎\| no_pins \| The number of O and IO pins. \|⏎\| #spec(data) \| Prefix and pin error data in binary format:<br><br>- The prefix is defined as follows:<br><br>- The number sign # marks the start of the data block.<br>- One digit follows, indicating how many digits make up the following data length specifier.<br>- A number of digits indicating the length (in bytes) of the data to follow.<br><br>- Binary data are available per reported cycle. The data for each cycle consists of two parts, the number of the failing cycle and the pin data, which indicates the failing pins of that cycle:<br><br>- Cycle number:<br><br>The length of the cycle number is always 8 bytes.<br><br>- Pin data:<br><br>The pin data consists of one byte per eight pins that belong to the specified port:<br><br>- Each pin is represented by one bit.<br>- An error on a particular pin is denoted by a 1, an error free pin by a 0.<br>- Bits of the pin data are filled starting from the left (MSB) side.<br>- If the number of configured channels of the specified port is not divisible by 8, the last byte of pin data contains unused bits on the right (LSB) side.<br><br>Mapping of the bits in the pin data block to the individual pins:<br><br>- The I / IO pins are sorted based on their pogo number defined for the first site.<br>- The sorted pins are mapped starting on the left (MSB) side of the first byte of the pin data block.<br>- The information how the pins are mapped can be read by command [FFPM? (First Failing cycle information Pin Mapping)](<02.06.11.02.24_FFPM___First_Failing_cycle_information_Pin_Mapping.md>). \|` |
| 291 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.11.02.24_FFPM___First_Failing_cycle_information_Pin_Mapping.md` | `144138.htm` | #0 | 普通 | `\| port \| Specifies the port for the pins for which information is to be returned. | 297 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.13.02.01_TSCF__TSCF__-__TMU_Set_ConFiguration.md` | `125467_2.htm` | #1 | 合并格 | `\| Property \| Explanation and values \| Explanation and values \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| DATA_RATE \| This property defines the data rate which is the smallest distance between two events. The unit is bits per second. \| This property defines the data rate which is the smallest distance between two events. The unit is bits per second. \|⏎\| EDGE_SELECT \| This mandatory property defines which edges are the events to be sampled. Possible values are: \| This mandatory property defines which edges are the events to be sampled. Possible values are: \|⏎\|  \| RISE \| The events are rising edges. \|⏎\|  \| FALL \| The events are falling edges. \|⏎\|  \| RISE_FALL \| The events are alternately rising and falling edges. This option is used for pulse and jitter measurements. If this option is selected, the PRESCALER property must be set to a value > 1 and < 17. \|⏎\| INITIAL_DISCARD \| This optional property defines how many events are to be discarded before the first sample of a measurement set. This refers to the events as they occur in the output signal of the prescaler. The default is 0.<br><br>If EDGE_SELECT is set to RISE_FALL, and INITIAL_DISCARD is set to an even number, the first sampled event will be a rising edge if INITIAL_DISCARD is set to an odd number, the first sampled event will be a falling edge.<br><br>Note: Parameter INITIAL_DISCARD must be 0 for DC Scale pins. \| This optional property defines how many events are to be discarded before the first sample of a measurement set. This refers to the events as they occur in the output signal of the prescaler. The default is 0.<br><br>If EDGE_SELECT is set to RISE_FALL, and INITIAL_DISCARD is set to an even number, the first sampled event will be a rising edge if INITIAL_DISCARD is set to an odd number, the first sampled event will be a falling edge.<br><br>Note: Parameter INITIAL_DISCARD must be 0 for DC Scale pins. \|⏎\| INTER_SAMPLE_DISCARD \| This optional property defines how many events are to be discarded between two samples of a measurement set. This refers to the events as they occur in the output signal of the prescaler. The default is 0.<br><br>If EDGE_SELECT is set to RISE_FALL, this property must be set to an even number (including 0). If it is set to an odd number, all sampled edges will be of the same type, all rising or all falling.<br><br>Note: Parameter INTER_SAMPLE_DISCARD must be 0 for DC Scale pins. \| This optional property defines how many events are to be discarded between two samples of a measurement set. This refers to the events as they occur in the output signal of the prescaler. The default is 0.<br><br>If EDGE_SELECT is set to RISE_FALL, this property must be set to an even number (including 0). If it is set to an odd number, all sampled edges will be of the same type, all rising or all falling.<br><br>Note: Parameter INTER_SAMPLE_DISCARD must be 0 for DC Scale pins. \|⏎\| MEAS_PER_SHOT \| This mandatory property must equal the number of armings in the pattern. A shot is one execution of the pattern. For certain measurements, two executions are required; see the NUM_SHOTS parameter.<br><br>This is one of the three parameters by which the required size of the result area is determined. The others are SAMPLES_PER_MEAS and NUM_SHOTS. \| This mandatory property must equal the number of armings in the pattern. A shot is one execution of the pattern. For certain measurements, two executions are required; see the NUM_SHOTS parameter.<br><br>This is one of the three parameters by which the required size of the result area is determined. The others are SAMPLES_PER_MEAS and NUM_SHOTS. \|⏎\| NUM_SHOTS \| This optional property defines the number of shots (sequencer runs). It is used to calculate the size of the result area and to determine how the measurements are to be processed. The possible values are 1 or 2. Two shots are needed for rise time and fall time measurements. The default is 1. \| This optional property defines the number of shots (sequencer runs). It is used to calculate the size of the result area and to determine how the measurements are to be processed. The possible values are 1 or 2. Two shots are needed for rise time and fall time measurements. The default is 1. \|⏎\| PRESCALER \| This mandatory property defines how much the frequency of the signal is to be reduced before it is passed on to the event discarding block. This is required if the minimal distance of two consecutive events in the DUT signal can be smaller than the minimal distance that the event discarding block supports.<br><br>Possible values for Smart Scale pins are 1 through 17. 1 means no prescaling. If EDGE_SELECT is set to RISE_FALL, the prescaler value must be < 17 and > 1. \| This mandatory property defines how much the frequency of the signal is to be reduced before it is passed on to the event discarding block. This is required if the minimal distance of two consecutive events in the DUT signal can be smaller than the minimal distance that the event discarding block supports.<br><br>Possible values for Smart Scale pins are 1 through 17. 1 means no prescaling. If EDGE_SELECT is set to RISE_FALL, the prescaler value must be < 17 and > 1. \|⏎\| SAMPLES_PER_MEAS \| This mandatory property defines the number of samples per measurement (arming). This is another parameter that contributes to the calculation of the result area size. \| This mandatory property defines the number of samples per measurement (arming). This is another parameter that contributes to the calculation of the result area size. \|⏎\| THRESHOLD_A \| This optional property defines a voltage threshold for the event to be sampled. It is only needed for (two shot) rise time and fall time measurements. The default is the voltage level as defined in the level setup.<br><br>Note that if this property is defined, the level setup must be changed, and if the pins specified by pinlist are tested functionally in parallel with the TMU measurement, they will be tested with the modified voltage level.<br><br>Note also that the original level setup is automatically restored after the measurement has been finished. \| This optional property defines a voltage threshold for the event to be sampled. It is only needed for (two shot) rise time and fall time measurements. The default is the voltage level as defined in the level setup.<br><br>Note that if this property is defined, the level setup must be changed, and if the pins specified by pinlist are tested functionally in parallel with the TMU measurement, they will be tested with the modified voltage level.<br><br>Note also that the original level setup is automatically restored after the measurement has been finished. \|⏎\| THRESHOLD_B \| This optional property defines the voltage threshold for a second shot. It is only needed for (two shot) rise time and fall time measurements.<br><br>It must not be defined if NUM_SHOTS is set to 1.<br><br>Note that the original level setup is automatically restored after the measurement has been finished. \| This optional property defines the voltage threshold for a second shot. It is only needed for (two shot) rise time and fall time measurements.<br><br>It must not be defined if NUM_SHOTS is set to 1.<br><br>Note that the original level setup is automatically restored after the measurement has been finished. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/125467_2.htm`。` |
| 298 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.15.03.04_TTDB__-__Transition_Test_DeBug_information.md` | `116688_2.htm` | #1 | 合并格 | `\| Information type \| Explanation and values \| Explanation and values \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| MAX_DEVIATION \| If the token size was > 0 during the last sequencer run, this item returns for this sequencer run:<br><br>- The maximum number of UI by which the received data has fallen behind the exceeded the expected data (subtype DUT_BEHIND_ED)<br>- The maximum number of UI by which the expected data has fallen behind the received data (subtype ED_BEHIND_DUT)<br><br>The received data is shortened by skipping idle tokens, the expected data by removing X-tokens. \| If the token size was > 0 during the last sequencer run, this item returns for this sequencer run:<br><br>- The maximum number of UI by which the received data has fallen behind the exceeded the expected data (subtype DUT_BEHIND_ED)<br>- The maximum number of UI by which the expected data has fallen behind the received data (subtype ED_BEHIND_DUT)<br><br>The received data is shortened by skipping idle tokens, the expected data by removing X-tokens. \|⏎\| TRK_FIRST_ADJUSTMENT \| This item returns the adjustment that was determined by the first transition after test start. \| This item returns the adjustment that was determined by the first transition after test start. \|⏎\| TRK_FIRST_ERR \| This item returns the number of expected bits that had been processed when the first tracking error occurred. \| This item returns the number of expected bits that had been processed when the first tracking error occurred. \|⏎\| TRK_VALUES \| This item returns information about the adjustment of the observation window. The unit is 0.25 UI (= 1 sample).<br><br>There are three subtypes:<br><br>- ACT returns the actual adjustment at the end of the test.<br>- MIN returns the maximum adjustment to the left during the last test.<br>- MAX returns the maximum adjustment to the right during the last test. \| This item returns information about the adjustment of the observation window. The unit is 0.25 UI (= 1 sample).<br><br>There are three subtypes:<br><br>- ACT returns the actual adjustment at the end of the test.<br>- MIN returns the maximum adjustment to the left during the last test.<br>- MAX returns the maximum adjustment to the right during the last test. \|⏎\| TRK_STATUS \| This item returns the transition patterns that were observed during the last test as a sequence of four-digit blocks.<br><br>In order to understand this information, you must take into account that the observation window can only be adjusted after every three UI (see the discussion of the implementation parameters in [Drift and transition tracking](<02.06.15.01.01_Drift_and_transition_tracking.md>)). Thus, the observation window actually consists of three successive UI.<br><br>The four-digit blocks encode the accumulated transitions for one such 3-UI window (subtype TRANS_COMPOSITE) or for two such windows (subtype TRANS_X). "1" indicates a transition, "0" indicates no transition. Every block starts with "1". This indicates that the observation window was adjusted successfully.<br><br>As an example for the one window case, consider<br><br>LLLL LHHH LLLH,<br><br>where the second UI has an AB transition, and the third has a CD transition. This is encoded by<br><br>1101,<br><br>which says that an AB transition (second digit) and a CD transition (fourth digit), but no BC transition was observed in the 3-UI window.<br><br>TRK_STATUS has three subtypes:<br><br>- TRANS_X returns the transition patterns that occurred in the last 24 UI (= 8 windows, each of 3 UI). Each of the four blocks accumulates two windows (= 6 UI). The order of the blocks corresponds to the order of the test.<br>- TRANS_COMPOSITE returns the transition patterns that occurred by accumulating the 3 UI of one window during the last sequencer run up to the first error. Since the first digit is always 1 (except when the window could not be adjusted) there are 8 possible patterns between 1000 (best) and 1111 (worst). The patterns that occurred are displayed.<br>- TRANS_R returns 0 if the observation window could always be adjusted successfully. It returns 1 if this is not the case. This error should only occur if the observation window was adjusted more than 1 UI in the same direction within 24 UI (see the ERR_CNTADJOVR property of the TTCF command). \| This item returns the transition patterns that were observed during the last test as a sequence of four-digit blocks.<br><br>In order to understand this information, you must take into account that the observation window can only be adjusted after every three UI (see the discussion of the implementation parameters in [Drift and transition tracking](<02.06.15.01.01_Drift_and_transition_tracking.md>)). Thus, the observation window actually consists of three successive UI.<br><br>The four-digit blocks encode the accumulated transitions for one such 3-UI window (subtype TRANS_COMPOSITE) or for two such windows (subtype TRANS_X). "1" indicates a transition, "0" indicates no transition. Every block starts with "1". This indicates that the observation window was adjusted successfully.<br><br>As an example for the one window case, consider<br><br>LLLL LHHH LLLH,<br><br>where the second UI has an AB transition, and the third has a CD transition. This is encoded by<br><br>1101,<br><br>which says that an AB transition (second digit) and a CD transition (fourth digit), but no BC transition was observed in the 3-UI window.<br><br>TRK_STATUS has three subtypes:<br><br>- TRANS_X returns the transition patterns that occurred in the last 24 UI (= 8 windows, each of 3 UI). Each of the four blocks accumulates two windows (= 6 UI). The order of the blocks corresponds to the order of the test.<br>- TRANS_COMPOSITE returns the transition patterns that occurred by accumulating the 3 UI of one window during the last sequencer run up to the first error. Since the first digit is always 1 (except when the window could not be adjusted) there are 8 possible patterns between 1000 (best) and 1111 (worst). The patterns that occurred are displayed.<br>- TRANS_R returns 0 if the observation window could always be adjusted successfully. It returns 1 if this is not the case. This error should only occur if the observation window was adjusted more than 1 UI in the same direction within 24 UI (see the ERR_CNTADJOVR property of the TTCF command). \|⏎\| LOCATION_FIRST_FAIL \| This item returns the number of expected bits that had been processed when the first error of any kind occurred. \| This item returns the number of expected bits that had been processed when the first error of any kind occurred. \|⏎\| REMOVED_IDLE \| This item returns how often one of the 6 idle tokens that had been defined with TTCF IDLE_TOKEN_i occurred during the last sequencer run. The sub_type parameter specifies the number of the token. \| This item returns how often one of the 6 idle tokens that had been defined with TTCF IDLE_TOKEN_i occurred during the last sequencer run. The sub_type parameter specifies the number of the token. \|⏎\| REMOVED_XTOKEN \| This item returns how many X-tokens were removed. This only applies if X-token removal is activated with TTCF REMOVE_XTOKEN_MODE.<br><br>REMOVED_XTOKEN has one subtype:<br><br>- SEQUENCER_RUN returns the number of X-tokens that were removed on the last sequencer run \| This item returns how many X-tokens were removed. This only applies if X-token removal is activated with TTCF REMOVE_XTOKEN_MODE.<br><br>REMOVED_XTOKEN has one subtype:<br><br>- SEQUENCER_RUN returns the number of X-tokens that were removed on the last sequencer run \|⏎\| STATUS \| This item returns information about the state of the tracker after the test as a bit sequence. Most of these status bits can also be queried with TTST?. The individual bits stand for: \| This item returns information about the state of the tracker after the test as a bit sequence. Most of these status bits can also be queried with TTST?. The individual bits stand for: \|⏎\| STATUS \| - Bit1: An interrupt was generated.<br>- Bit2: Break vectors entered the formatting block.<br>- Bit3: The first match between expected and received data has occurred.<br>- Bit4: Expected and received data are prepared for the first compare.<br>- Bit5: The FIFO between tracking and pattern matching block is empty.<br>- Bit6: The FIFO between expected data and pattern matching block is empty.<br>- Bit7: The expected data window FIFO is empty.<br>- Bit8: The expected data FIFO is empty.<br>- Bit9: EXPECTED_FIFO_DUT_OVERFLOW<br>- Bit10: ADJUSTMENT_ERROR<br>- Bit11: Always 1<br>- Bit12: DRIVE_BIT_IN_COMPARE_DATA<br>- Bit13: DUT_FIFO_OVERFLOW<br>- Bit14: ERR_XCNT \| - Bit15: ERR_XMIN<br>- Bit16: ERR_XMAX<br>- Bit17: ERR_TRMCNT<br>- Bit18: ERR_WRCMP<br>- Bit19: ERR_COMPARE<br>- Bit20: ERR_STO<br>- Bit21: ERR_FTO<br>- Bit22: ERR_STOP<br>- Bit23: ERR_CNTLMT<br>- Bit24: ERR_CNTADJOVR<br>- Bit25: ERR_BADTRAN<br>- Bit26: ERR_PULSE<br>- Bit27: ERR_TRK<br>- Bit28: ERR_JMP2<br>- Bit29: ERR_TE4<br>- Bit30: ERR_TE0<br>- Bit31: ERR_TE2<br>- Bit32: ERR_TE3 \|⏎\| STATUS \| The names specified for bits 9 through 32 refer to the possible errors returned by [TTST?](<02.06.02.02.292_TTST__-__Transition_Test_STatus_information.md>). For more explanations see this query and the [TTCF](<02.06.02.02.286_TTCF__TTCF__-__Transition_Test_ConFiguration.md>) command. \| The names specified for bits 9 through 32 refer to the possible errors returned by [TTST?](<02.06.02.02.292_TTST__-__Transition_Test_STatus_information.md>). For more explanations see this query and the [TTCF](<02.06.02.02.286_TTCF__TTCF__-__Transition_Test_ConFiguration.md>) command. \|⏎\| REMOVED_XTOKEN_EMAP \| This item returns how many x-tokens were removed with respect to start and stop of the error map.<br><br>REMOVED_XTOKEN_EMAP has two subtypes:<br><br>- START returns the number of removed X-tokens at the start of the error map.<br>- STOP returns the number of removed X-tokens at the end of error map recording. \| This item returns how many x-tokens were removed with respect to start and stop of the error map.<br><br>REMOVED_XTOKEN_EMAP has two subtypes:<br><br>- START returns the number of removed X-tokens at the start of the error map.<br>- STOP returns the number of removed X-tokens at the end of error map recording. \|⏎\| SYNC_VALUES \| This item returns how many received bits were skipped for initial synchronization at test start (subtype INITIAL) or after a switch of an IO pin from drive to receive mode (subtype SUBSEQUENT). \| This item returns how many received bits were skipped for initial synchronization at test start (subtype INITIAL) or after a switch of an IO pin from drive to receive mode (subtype SUBSEQUENT). \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/116688_2.htm`。` |
| 299 | `02_System_reference/02.06_Command_Reference/02.06.16.02.07_MSCF__MSCF__-__Modulation_Set_ConFiguration.md` | `143671_2.htm` | #1 | 合并格 | `\| Property \| Explanation and values \| Explanation and values \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| SHAPE \| This parameter either<br><br>(a) defines a list of offsets (in picoseconds) from the edge delays specified in the currently active timing set, or<br><br>(b) selects a predefined waveform.<br><br>In case (a), the list items must be separated by a comma, and the maximum number of items is 3072. Note that case (a) is only supported for STDDIFF pins.<br><br>The list items of case (a) do not define the actual delays, but only their mutual proportions. The actual values are computed from the list and the required maximum value (AMPLITUDE property) by<br><br>list_item * ( max_value / max_item),<br><br>where max_item is the greatest item in the specified list. To give a simple example, if the specified list is<br><br>0, 10, 20, 10, 0, -5, -10, -5, 0<br><br>and the required maximum value is 30ps, then the actual waveform is calculated by<br><br>list_item * ( 30 / 20)<br><br>as<br><br>0, 15, 30, 15, 0, -7.5, -15, -7.5, 0<br><br>picoseconds. If no amplitude is specified, the default of 100ps is assumed.<br><br>The predefined waveforms (case (b)) are: \| This parameter either<br><br>(a) defines a list of offsets (in picoseconds) from the edge delays specified in the currently active timing set, or<br><br>(b) selects a predefined waveform.<br><br>In case (a), the list items must be separated by a comma, and the maximum number of items is 3072. Note that case (a) is only supported for STDDIFF pins.<br><br>The list items of case (a) do not define the actual delays, but only their mutual proportions. The actual values are computed from the list and the required maximum value (AMPLITUDE property) by<br><br>list_item * ( max_value / max_item),<br><br>where max_item is the greatest item in the specified list. To give a simple example, if the specified list is<br><br>0, 10, 20, 10, 0, -5, -10, -5, 0<br><br>and the required maximum value is 30ps, then the actual waveform is calculated by<br><br>list_item * ( 30 / 20)<br><br>as<br><br>0, 15, 30, 15, 0, -7.5, -15, -7.5, 0<br><br>picoseconds. If no amplitude is specified, the default of 100ps is assumed.<br><br>The predefined waveforms (case (b)) are: \|⏎\|  \| SINE \| Creates a sinusoidal waveform. \|⏎\|  \| RANDOM \| Generates random jitter within delay - amplitude and delay + amplitude,<br><br>where delay is the delay that is defined in the currently active timing set for the pins that are associated with the modulation set in the respective jitter set, and amplitude is the value of the AMPLITUDE property. \|⏎\| PHASE \| This property is intended to define a phase offset for the RESTART mode (the mode is defined with JSCF). This value must be specified as a degree. The maximum is 360°. \| This property is intended to define a phase offset for the RESTART mode (the mode is defined with JSCF). This value must be specified as a degree. The maximum is 360°. \|⏎\| AMPLITUDE \| This property determines the amplitude (that is, half of the peak-to-peak value) of a predefined shape.<br><br>The maximum is 200 ps. The default is always 100 ps. \| This property determines the amplitude (that is, half of the peak-to-peak value) of a predefined shape.<br><br>The maximum is 200 ps. The default is always 100 ps. \|⏎\| PERIOD \| This property defines the jitter period in picoseconds.<br><br>In general, the maximum period depends on the clock resolution as follows:<br><br>2 * clock_resolution * 1024 * 512<br><br>The clock resolution is calculated from the period resolution, but also takes other parameters into account. Therefore a different clock resolution may be calculated from the same period resolution in different circumstances.<br><br>If you want to have complete control over the clock resolution, you must specify a period resolution not greater than 2 * minimal_clock_resolution manually (with the period_res keyword). Then the clock resolution will equal the period resolution. See also [Period resolution, clock resolution, and master clock period](<02.06.06.01.04.01.02.03_Period_resolution__clock_resolution__and_master_clock_period.md>). \| This property defines the jitter period in picoseconds.<br><br>In general, the maximum period depends on the clock resolution as follows:<br><br>2 * clock_resolution * 1024 * 512<br><br>The clock resolution is calculated from the period resolution, but also takes other parameters into account. Therefore a different clock resolution may be calculated from the same period resolution in different circumstances.<br><br>If you want to have complete control over the clock resolution, you must specify a period resolution not greater than 2 * minimal_clock_resolution manually (with the period_res keyword). Then the clock resolution will equal the period resolution. See also [Period resolution, clock resolution, and master clock period](<02.06.06.01.04.01.02.03_Period_resolution__clock_resolution__and_master_clock_period.md>). \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/143671_2.htm`。` |

| 310 | `02_System_reference/02.07_Test_Method_Reference/02.07.04.01.114_GPIB_SEND.md` | `29475.htm` | #2 | 合并格 | `\| Mnemonic \| Decimal Value \| Description \|⏎\|---\|---\|---\|⏎\| Universal Commands \| Universal Commands \| Universal Commands \|⏎\| GPIB_UNL \| 63 \| (Unlisten) Unaddresses all present listeners on the GPIB. You cannot unaddress a specific listener without unaddressing all listeners on the GPIB. This command ensures that the GPIB is cleared of all listeners before addressing a new listener or group of listeners. \|⏎\| GPIB_UNT \| 95 \| (Untalk) Unaddresses a present talker on the GPIB. This command ensures that the GPIB is cleared of a talker before addressing a new talker. \|⏎\| GPIB_DCL \| 20 \| (Device Clear) Resets all devices to device-dependent states. \|⏎\| GPIB_LLO \| 17 \| (Local Lock Out) Locks out local buttons of all devices. \|⏎\| GPIB_SPE \| 24 \| (Serial Poll Enable) Establishes serial poll mode for all devices that are capable of being GPIB talkers, provided they recognize and support the command. This command operates independently of whether the devices being polled have been addressed to talk. When a device is addressed to talk, it returns an 8-bit status byte message. \|⏎\| GPIB_SPD \| 25 \| (Serial Poll Disable) Terminates serial poll mode for all devices. \|⏎\| GPIB_PPU \| 21 \| (Parallel Poll Unconfigure) Unconfigures parallel poll configuration of all devices. \|⏎\| Addressed Commands \| Addressed Commands \| Addressed Commands \|⏎\| GPIB_GET \| 08 \| (Group Execute Trigger) Tells devices currently addressed as listeners to initiate some device-dependent action. \|⏎\| GPIB_SDC \| 04 \| (Selected Device Clear) Resets devices currently addressed as listeners to device-dependent states. \|⏎\| GPIB_GTL \| 01 \| (Go To Local) Returns all devices currently addressed as listeners to their local state. \|⏎\| GPIB_PPC \| 05 \| (Parallel Poll Configure) Tells devices currently addressed as listeners that a secondary command follows. The secondary command must be either Parallel Poll Enable or Parallel Poll Disable. \|⏎\| GPIB_TCT \| 09 \| (Take Control) Passes control of GPIB from present active controller to another device. This device must be capable of becoming an active controller, which usually means it is a computer. \|⏎\| Talk and Listen Addresses \| Talk and Listen Addresses \| Talk and Listen Addresses \|⏎\| GPIB_TA0 - GPIB_TA30 \| 64-94 \| (Talk Address Group) Addresses a talker. \|⏎\| GPIB_LA0 - GPIB_LA30 \| 32-62 \| (Listen Address Group) Addresses a listener. \|⏎\| Secondary Commands \| Secondary Commands \| Secondary Commands \|⏎\| GPIB_SC0 - GPIB_SC31 \| 96-127 \| (Secondary Command Group) Secondary commands can be used only with another command. Its meaning is device dependent. \|⏎\| (If a secondary command follows the Parallel Poll Configure command, then it is interpreted as follows) \| (If a secondary command follows the Parallel Poll Configure command, then it is interpreted as follows) \| (If a secondary command follows the Parallel Poll Configure command, then it is interpreted as follows) \|⏎\| GPIB_SC0 - GPIB_SC15 \| 96-111 \| (Parallel Poll enable) Configures devices to respond to parallel polls on a particular data line. The response is device dependent.<br><br>This command must be preceded by GPIB_PPC. \|⏎\| GPIB_SC16 \| 112 \| (Parallel Poll Disable) Prevents devices from responding to parallel poll. \|⏎⏎> ⚠️ 转录说明（非原文）：本表在上游 HTML 里含合并格（colspan / rowspan）；⏎> 跨格文字已按覆盖范围重复写出（**不是原表里有重复的格子**）。真结构见 `tdc-html/raw/29475.htm`。` |
